| (6.26) |  |  | =infθ∈𝒯\[0,1\](T−t)𝔼⁡\[∫0θ(T−t)​c​(t+(T−t)​u)​𝑑u+g~​(T−t​Yθt,l/T−t)\]\\displaystyle=\\inf\_{\\theta\\in\\mathcal{T}^{(T-t)}\_{\[0,1\]}}\\mathbb{E}\\left\[\\int\_{0}^{\\theta}(T-t)c(t+(T-t)u)\\,du+\\tilde{g}(\\sqrt{T-t}Y\_{\\theta}^{t,l/\\sqrt{T-t}})\\right\] |  |\
\
and Yst,i:=Ls⁡(T−t)i​T−t/T−tY\_{s}^{t,i}:=L\_{s(T-t)}^{i\\sqrt{T-t}}/\\sqrt{T-t}, which solves\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | Yst,i=i+∫0sT−t​μ​(T−t​Yut,i)​𝑑u+WstY\_{s}^{t,i}=i+\\int\_{0}^{s}\\sqrt{T-t}\\mu(\\sqrt{T-t}Y\_{u}^{t,i})\\,du+W^{t}\_{s} |  |\
\
where Wst:=Bs⁡(T−t)/T−tW^{t}\_{s}:=B\_{s(T-t)}/\\sqrt{T-t}, so (Wst)s∈\[0,1\](W^{t}\_{s})\_{s\\in\[0,1\]} is a Brownian motion.\
There is a one-to-one correspondence between the optimal stopping times of ( [6.24](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E24 "In Proof. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")) and ( [6.26](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E26 "In Proof. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")).\
\
Consider a new Wiener space (Ω,𝒢,𝔾,ℚ)(\\Omega,\\mathcal{G},\\mathbb{G},\\mathbb{Q}) carrying a Brownian motion W=(Wt)t≥0W=(W\_{t})\_{t\\geq 0}, and define the process It,iI^{t,i} as the unique strong solution to\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | Ist,i=i+∫0sT−t​μ​(T−t​Iut,i)​𝑑u+Ws.I\_{s}^{t,i}=i+\\int\_{0}^{s}\\sqrt{T-t}\\mu(\\sqrt{T-t}I\_{u}^{t,i})\\,du+W\_{s}. |  |\
\
Letting 𝒯\[0,1\]\\mathcal{T}\_{\[0,1\]} be the stopping times with respect to 𝔾\\mathbb{G} restricted to \[0,1\]\[0,1\], we can consider the auxiliary optimal stopping problem\

|     |     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- | --- |\
| (6.27) |  | VI​(t,i):=infθ∈𝒯\[0,1\]𝔼ℚ​\[∫0θ(T−t)​c​(t+(T−t)​u)​𝑑u+g~​(T−t​Iθt,i)\].V^{I}(t,i):=\\inf\_{\\theta\\in\\mathcal{T}\_{\[0,1\]}}\\mathbb{E}^{\\mathbb{Q}}\\left\[\\int\_{0}^{\\theta}(T-t)c(t+(T-t)u)\\,du+\\tilde{g}(\\sqrt{T-t}I\_{\\theta}^{t,i})\\right\]. |  |\
\
By a similar argument to Proposition [3.7](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S3.Thmtheorem7 "Proposition 3.7. ‣ 3.5. Preliminary Analysis ‣ 3. Note that distributional equivalence yields VI​(t,i)=V~T​(t,i​T−t)V^{I}(t,i)=\\tilde{V}\_{T}(t,i\\sqrt{T-t}), and so the optimal stopping time is given by\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | θ∗​(t,i)=inf{s∈\[0,1):(t+(T−t)​s,T−t​Ist,i)∈𝒟~T}∧1.\\theta^{\*}(t,i)=\\inf\\{s\\in\[0,1):(t+(T-t)s,\\sqrt{T-t}I\_{s}^{t,i})\\in\\tilde{\\mathcal{D}}\_{T}\\}\\wedge 1. |  |\
\
Thus again by the distributional equivalence of paths, we may conclude that (It,i,θ∗)(I^{t,i},\\theta^{\*}) is equal in law to (Yt,i,τ~∗/(T−t))(Y^{t,i},\\tilde{\\tau}^{\*}/(T-t)) for τ~∗:=τ~T∗​(t,i​T−t)\\tilde{\\tau}^{\*}:=\\tilde{\\tau}^{\*}\_{T}(t,i\\sqrt{T-t}). Furthermore, by the argument around ( [6.11](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E11 "In 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")), we have\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | Ist,i∈Ψ⁡(\[A∗​(T),B∗​(T)\])T−t⊂Ψ⁡((,,,))T−tI\_{s}^{t,i}\\in\\frac{\\Psi(\[A^{\*}(T),B^{\*}(T)\])}{\\sqrt{T-t}}\\subset\\frac{\\Psi((0,1))}{\\sqrt{T-t}} |  |\
\
for all s≤θ∗​(t,i)s\\leq\\theta^{\*}(t,i) when ii is within the interval and similarly in the Lamperti and original coordinates. In the following analysis we restrict to such an interval, which ensures boundedness by Assumption [B.(i)](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S3.I2.i1 "In Assumption B (Extended assumptions). ‣ 3.2. Assumptions ‣ 3. Setup and Preliminary Analysis ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales").\
\
Differentiating the flow t↦Ist,it\\mapsto I\_{s}^{t,i} as in ( [3.12](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S3.E12 "In 3.4. Transformations and subproblems ‣ 3. \
|     |     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- | --- |\
|  | ∂tIt,is=∫0s\\displaystyle\\partial\_{t}I^{t,i}\_{s}=\\int\_{0}^{s} | −12​T−t​μ​(T−t​Iut,i)−12​μ′​(T−t​Iut,i)​Iut,i⏞η⁡(t,Iut,i)\\displaystyle\\quad\\overbrace{-\\frac{1}{2\\sqrt{T-t}}\\mu(\\sqrt{T-t}I^{t,i}\_{u})-\\frac{1}{2}\\mu^{\\prime}(\\sqrt{T-t}I^{t,i}\_{u})I^{t,i}\_{u}}^{\\eta(t,I^{t,i}\_{u})} |  |\
|  |  | +(T−t)μ′(T−tIut,i)∂tIut,idu.\\displaystyle+(T-t)\\mu^{\\prime}(\\sqrt{T-t}I^{t,i}\_{u})\\partial\_{t}I^{t,i}\_{u}\\,du. |  |\
\
Defining Z⁡(s)=∂tIst,iZ(s)=\\partial\_{t}I^{t,i}\_{s}, we get the equivalent differential equation Z⁡(0)=0Z(0)=0 and\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | Z′​(s)=η⁡(t,Ist,i)+(T−t)​μ′​(T−t​Ist,i)​Z​(s)Z^{\\prime}(s)=\\eta(t,I^{t,i}\_{s})+(T-t)\\mu^{\\prime}(\\sqrt{T-t}I^{t,i}\_{s})Z(s) |  |\
\
i.e.\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | ∂tIst,i=∫0sη⁡(t,Iut,i)​e∫us(T−t)​μ′​(T−t​Ivt,i)​𝑑v​𝑑u.\\partial\_{t}I^{t,i}\_{s}=\\int\_{0}^{s}\\eta(t,I^{t,i}\_{u})e^{\\int\_{u}^{s}(T-t)\\mu^{\\prime}(\\sqrt{T-t}I^{t,i}\_{v})dv}\\,du. |  |\
\
Similarly, for the flow i↦Ist,ii\\mapsto I^{t,i}\_{s} we get\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | ∂iIst,i=exp⁡{∫0s(T−t)​μ′​(T−t​Ivt,i)​𝑑v}.\\partial\_{i}I^{t,i}\_{s}=\\exp\\left\\{\\int\_{0}^{s}(T-t)\\mu^{\\prime}(\\sqrt{T-t}I^{t,i}\_{v})dv\\right\\}. |  |\
\
The function (t,i)↦VI​(t,i)=V~T​(t,i​T−t)(t,i)\\mapsto V^{I}(t,i)=\\tilde{V}\_{T}(t,i\\sqrt{T-t}) is locally Lipschitz by an argument similar to Theorem [6.7](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem7 "Theorem 6.7. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales"). Let (t,i)(t,i) be a point of differentiability. For fixed θ\\theta, let J⁡(t,i,θ)J(t,i;\\theta) denote the objective in ( [6.27](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E27 "In Proof. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")). If θ∗\\theta^{\*} is optimal at (t,i)(t,i), then localization to a compact interval strictly containing the homogeneous bound and dominated convergence show that J⁡(⋅,⋅,θ∗)J(\\cdot,\\cdot;\\theta^{\*}) is differentiable at (t,i)(t,i). For h>0h>0,\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | VI​(t+h,i)−VI​(t,i)h≤J⁡(t+h,i,θ∗)−J⁡(t,i,θ∗)h,\\frac{V^{I}(t+h,i)-V^{I}(t,i)}{h}\\leq\\frac{J(t+h,i;\\theta^{\*})-J(t,i;\\theta^{\*})}{h}, |  |\
\
while division by h<0h<0 reverses the inequality. At a differentiability point the two one-sided limits agree, so ∂tVI​(t,i)=∂tJ⁡(t,i,θ∗)\\partial\_{t}V^{I}(t,i)=\\partial\_{t}J(t,i;\\theta^{\*}). The same argument applies to the spatial derivative. Applying these identities and differentiating the objective in ( [6.27](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E27 "In Proof. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")), we get\
\
|     |     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- | --- |\
|  | ∂tVI(t,i)=𝔼ℚ\[\\displaystyle\\partial\_{t}V^{I}(t,i)=\\mathbb{E}^{\\mathbb{Q}}\\Big\[ | ∫0θ∗(T−t)​c′​(t+(T−t)​u)​(1−u)−c⁡(t+(T−t)​u)​𝑑u\\displaystyle\\int\_{0}^{\\theta^{\*}}(T-t)c^{\\prime}(t+(T-t)u)(1-u)-c(t+(T-t)u)\\,du |  |\
|  |  | +g~′(T−tIt,iθ∗)(T−t∂tIt,iθ∗−Iθ∗t,i2​T−t)\]\\displaystyle+\\tilde{g}^{\\prime}(\\sqrt{T-t}I^{t,i}\_{\\theta^{\*}})\\left(\\sqrt{T-t}\\partial\_{t}I^{t,i}\_{\\theta^{\*}}-\\frac{I^{t,i}\_{\\theta^{\*}}}{2\\sqrt{T-t}}\\right)\\Big\] |  |\
\
where θ∗\\theta^{\*} is the optimal stopping time for initial data (t,i)(t,i), and we note that the integral term can be simplified using integration by parts,\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | ∫0s(T−t)​c′​(t+(T−t)​u)​(1−u)​𝑑u=∫0s⁡(T−t)c′​(t+u)​𝑑u−c⁡(t+s⁡(T−t))​s+∫0sc⁡(t+(T−t)​u)​𝑑u.\\int\_{0}^{s}(T-t)c^{\\prime}(t+(T-t)u)(1-u)\\,du=\\int\_{0}^{s(T-t)}c^{\\prime}(t+u)\\,du-c(t+s(T-t))s+\\int\_{0}^{s}c(t+(T-t)u)\\,du. |  |\
\
Similarly, for the spatial derivative of VIV^{I} we get\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | ∂iVI​(t,i)=𝔼ℚ​\[g~′​(T−t​Iθ∗t,i)​(T−t​∂iIθ∗t,i)\].\\partial\_{i}V^{I}(t,i)=\\mathbb{E}^{\\mathbb{Q}}\\left\[\\tilde{g}^{\\prime}(\\sqrt{T-t}I^{t,i}\_{\\theta^{\*}})\\left(\\sqrt{T-t}\\partial\_{i}I^{t,i}\_{\\theta^{\*}}\\right)\\right\]. |  |\
\
To revert the transformation,\
we first note that by the chain rule away from TT, we have\
\
|     |     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- | --- |\
|  |  | ∂tV~T​(t,l)=∂tVI​(t,lT−t)+l2​(T−t)3/2​∂iVI​(t,lT−t)\\displaystyle\\partial\_{t}\\tilde{V}\_{T}(t,l)=\\partial\_{t}V^{I}\\left(t,\\frac{l}{\\sqrt{T-t}}\\right)+\\frac{l}{2(T-t)^{3/2}}\\partial\_{i}V^{I}\\left(t,\\frac{l}{\\sqrt{T-t}}\\right) |  |\
|  | =\\displaystyle= | 𝔼ℚ\[g~′(T−tIθ∗t,i)(T−t∂tIθ∗t,i−Iθ∗t,i2​T−t+l2​(T−t)∂iIθ∗t,i)−c(t+θ∗(T−t))θ∗\\displaystyle\\mathbb{E}^{\\mathbb{Q}}\\Bigg\[\\tilde{g}^{\\prime}(\\sqrt{T-t}I^{t,i}\_{\\theta^{\*}})\\left(\\sqrt{T-t}\\partial\_{t}I^{t,i}\_{\\theta^{\*}}-\\frac{I^{t,i}\_{\\theta^{\*}}}{2\\sqrt{T-t}}+\\frac{l}{2(T-t)}\\partial\_{i}I^{t,i}\_{\\theta^{\*}}\\right)-c(t+\\theta^{\*}(T-t))\\theta^{\*} |  |\
|  |  | +∫0θ∗​(T−t)c′(t+u)du\],\\displaystyle\\qquad+\\int\_{0}^{\\theta^{\*}(T-t)}c^{\\prime}(t+u)\\,du\\Bigg\], |  |\
\
where i=l/T−ti=l/\\sqrt{T-t}.\
Furthermore, recalling ( [3.12](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S3.E12 "In 3.4. Transformations and subproblems ‣ 3. Setup and Preliminary Analysis ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")), we note that for fixed tt the quantity ∂iIst,l/T−t\\partial\_{i}I^{t,l/\\sqrt{T-t}}\_{s} has the same law under ℚ\\mathbb{Q} as ∂lLs⁡(T−t)l\\partial\_{l}L^{l}\_{s(T-t)} has under ℙ\\mathbb{P}. Similarly, ∂tIst,l/T−t\\partial\_{t}I^{t,l/\\sqrt{T-t}}\_{s} has the same law under ℚ\\mathbb{Q} as\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | Zs⁡(T−t)l:=−12​(T−t)3/2∫0s⁡(T−t)∂lLs⁡(T−t)l∂lLul(μ(Lul)+Lulμ′(Lul))duZ^{l}\_{s(T-t)}:=-\\frac{1}{2(T-t)^{3/2}}\\int\_{0}^{s(T-t)}\\frac{\\partial\_{l}L^{l}\_{s(T-t)}}{\\partial\_{l}L^{l}\_{u}}\\left(\\mu(L^{l}\_{u})+L^{l}\_{u}\\mu^{\\prime}(L^{l}\_{u})\\right)\\,du |  |\
\
has under ℙ\\mathbb{P}. This can indeed be extended to path laws, jointly with the optimal stopping time. Namely, (It,i,∂iIt,i,∂tIt,i,θ∗)(I^{t,i},\\partial\_{i}I^{t,i},\\partial\_{t}I^{t,i},\\theta^{\*}) under ℚ\\mathbb{Q} has the same law as\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | (L⋅(T−t)lT−t,∂lL⋅(T−t)l,Z⋅(T−t)l,τ~∗T−t)\\left(\\frac{L^{l}\_{\\cdot(T-t)}}{\\sqrt{T-t}},\\partial\_{l}L^{l}\_{\\cdot(T-t)},Z^{l}\_{\\cdot(T-t)},\\frac{\\tilde{\\tau}^{\*}}{T-t}\\right) |  |\
\
has under ℙ\\mathbb{P}.\
Thus, we can express ∂tV~T​(t,l)\\partial\_{t}\\tilde{V}\_{T}(t,l) by\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | ∂tV~T​(t,l)=𝔼⁡\[c⁡(t+τ∗)\]−c⁡(t)−1T−t​𝔼​\[c⁡(t+τ∗)​τ∗+12​g~′​(Lτ∗l)​∂lLτ∗l​Ξτ∗\],\\partial\_{t}\\tilde{V}\_{T}(t,l)=\\mathbb{E}\[c(t+\\tau^{\*})\]-c(t)-\\frac{1}{T-t}\\mathbb{E}\\left\[c(t+\\tau^{\*})\\tau^{\*}+\\frac{1}{2}\\tilde{g}^{\\prime}(L^{l}\_{\\tau^{\*}})\\partial\_{l}L^{l}\_{\\tau^{\*}}\\Xi\_{\\tau^{\*}}\\right\], |  |\
\
where Ξ\\Xi is given by\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | Ξt=Ltl∂lLtl−l+∫0tμ⁡(Lul)+Lul​μ′​(Lul)∂lLul​𝑑u=2​∫0tμ⁡(Lul)∂lLul​𝑑u+∫0t1∂lLul​d​Bu,\\Xi\_{t}=\\frac{L^{l}\_{t}}{\\partial\_{l}L^{l}\_{t}}-l+\\int\_{0}^{t}\\frac{\\mu(L^{l}\_{u})+L^{l}\_{u}\\mu^{\\prime}(L^{l}\_{u})}{\\partial\_{l}L^{l}\_{u}}\\,du=2\\int\_{0}^{t}\\frac{\\mu(L^{l}\_{u})}{\\partial\_{l}L^{l}\_{u}}\\,du+\\int\_{0}^{t}\\frac{1}{\\partial\_{l}L^{l}\_{u}}dB\_{u}, |  |\
\
and we again note that the preceding inclusion implies bounded coefficients for Ξ\\Xi on s≤τ~∗s\\leq\\tilde{\\tau}^{\*}.\
Rewriting in terms of ∂tVT​(t,x)=∂tV~T​(t,Ψ⁡(x))\\partial\_{t}V\_{T}(t,x)=\\partial\_{t}\\tilde{V}\_{T}(t,\\Psi(x)), we get the claim.\
∎\
\
The probabilistic representation of the temporal derivative is nice but does not play a role in the following analysis of the optimal stopping problem, since the bound in Corollary [6.9](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem9 "Corollary 6.9. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales") is easier to control. However, it does enter into the probabilistic representation of the second spatial derivative as seen in Corollary [4.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S4.Thmtheorem3 "Corollary 4.3. ‣ 4. Main Results ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales").\
\
Before going on to the regularity of the optimal stopping boundary, we conclude this section with a classical result on the regularity of the value function.\
\
###### Proposition 6.11.\
\
The value function VTV\_{T} satisfies\
\
|     |     |     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\
| (6.28) |  | (∂t+ℒ)VT(t,x)=−c(t),\\displaystyle(\\partial\_{t}+\\mathcal{L})V\_{T}(t,x)=-c(t),\\qquad | (t,x)∈𝒞T,\\displaystyle(t,x)\\in\\mathcal{C}\_{T}, |  |\
| (6.29) |  | VT​(t,x)=g​(x),\\displaystyle V\_{T}(t,x)=g(x),\\qquad | (t,x)∈𝒟T,\\displaystyle(t,x)\\in\\mathcal{D}\_{T}, |  |\
\
for any T≤∞T\\leq\\infty.\
Specifically, the functions ∂tVT,∂xVT\\partial\_{t}V\_{T},\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}, and ∂x​xVT\\partial\_{xx}V\_{T} are jointly continuous in (t,x)∈𝒞T(t,x)\\in\\mathcal{C}\_{T}.\
\
###### Proof.\
\
Since 𝒞T\\mathcal{C}\_{T} is open by Proposition [3.7](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S3.Thmtheorem7 "Proposition 3.7. ‣ 3.5. Preliminary Analysis ‣ 3. Setup and Preliminary Analysis ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales") and (t,x)↦VT​(t,x)(t,x)\\mapsto V\_{T}(t,x) is continuous by Theorem [6.7](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem7 "Theorem 6.7. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales"), the PDE ( [6.28](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E28 "In Proposition 6.11. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")) follows from standard Markovian optimal stopping arguments applied locally; see for example the methodology in \[ [24](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#bib.bib24 ""), Theorem 2.7.7\]. The condition ( [6.29](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E29 "In Proposition 6.11. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")) follows from the definition of 𝒟T\\mathcal{D}\_{T}.\
∎\
\
Equipped with Proposition [6.11](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem11 "Proposition 6.11. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales") we can specify the points of differentiability of VTV\_{T}, namely, VTV\_{T} is differentiable away from the boundary. This indeed also yields the first part of Corollary [4.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S4.Thmtheorem3 "Corollary 4.3. ‣ 4. Main Results ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales").\
\
###### Corollary 6.12.\
\
The second spatial derivative of the value function admits the representation\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | ∂x​xVT​(t,x)={−2​c⁡(t)+∂tVT​(t,x)σ2​(x)for ​(t,x)∈𝒞Tg′′​(x)for ​(t,x)∈(𝒟T)∘.\\partial\_{xx}V\_{T}(t,x)=\\begin{cases}-2\\frac{c(t)+\\partial\_{t}V\_{T}(t,x)}{\\sigma^{2}(x)}&\\text{for }(t,x)\\in\\mathcal{C}\_{T}\\\<br>g^{\\prime\\prime}(x)&\\text{for }(t,x)\\in(\\mathcal{D}\_{T})^{\\circ}.\\end{cases} |  |\
\
###### Proof.\
\
The expression for ∂x​xVT​(t,x)\\partial\_{xx}V\_{T}(t,x) in (𝒟T)∘(\\mathcal{D}\_{T})^{\\circ} is obvious.\
In the continuation region the value function VTV\_{T} obeys the partial differential equation ( [6.28](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E28 "In Proposition 6.11. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")), and thus the expression for ∂x​xVT\\partial\_{xx}V\_{T} in 𝒞T\\mathcal{C}\_{T} can easily be obtained.\
∎\
\
### 6.2. Properties of the optimal stopping boundary\
\
Next, we investigate the structure of the continuation set. We first show that the continuation set, and thus the optimal stopping boundary, can be described by at most two functions, partially showing Theorem [4.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S4.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem 4.1 (Structure of the continuation region). ‣ 4. Main Results ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales").\
\
###### Proposition 6.13.\
\
Let T≤∞T\\leq\\infty. There exists a lower semicontinuous function bT:\[0,T)→\[1/2,1\]b\_{T}:\[0,T)\\to\[1/2,1\] such that the continuation region is given by\

|     |     |     |     |\

| (6.30) |  | 𝒞T={(t,x)∈𝒮T:1−bT​(t)<x<bT​(t)}.\\mathcal{C}\_{T}=\\{(t,x)\\in\\mathcal{S}\_{T}:1-b\_{T}(t)<x<b\_{T}(t)\\}. |  |\

Furthermore, bT​(t)≥γ⁡(t)b\_{T}(t)\\geq\\gamma(t) for any t∈\[0,T)t\\in\[0,T).\
\
###### Proof.\
\
First, let T<∞T<\\infty. We prove the equivalent statement in the Lamperti coordinates.\
Note first that ℒ~​g~​(l)=ℒ​g​(Ψ−1​(l))\\tilde{\\mathcal{L}}\\tilde{g}(l)=\\mathcal{L}g(\\Psi^{-1}(l)), and hence, by Assumption [A.(iv)](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S3.I1.i4 "In Assumption A (Baseline assumptions). ‣ 3.2. Recall the definition of the extended continuation set 𝒞^T\\hat{\\mathcal{C}}\_{T}. while from Proposition [6.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem1 "Proposition 6.1. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. ∎\
\
###### Remark 6.14.\
\
We note that this argument is specialized to smooth symmetric costs gg. For the classical hard-classification cost g⁡(x)=x∧(1−x)g(x)=x\\wedge(1-x), the existence of a boundary function instead follows directly from the concavity of VTV\_{T}, since gg is affine on each side of 1/21/2 and VT​(t,⋅)≤gV\_{T}(t,\\cdot)\\leq g with equality at the endpoints..\
\
Proposition [6.13](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem13 "Proposition 6.13. ‣ 6.2. Properties of the optimal stopping boundary ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales") also showed ∂lW~T​(t,l)≥0\\partial\_{l}\\tilde{W}\_{T}(t,l)\\geq 0 for l≥0l\\geq 0 and T<∞T<\\infty. We need a stronger result for the subsequent analysis.\
\
###### Lemma 6.15.\
\
Let T<∞T<\\infty. If (t,l)∈𝒞~T(t,l)\\in\\tilde{\\mathcal{C}}\_{T} with l>0l>0, then ∂lW~T​(t,l)>0\\partial\_{l}\\tilde{W}\_{T}(t,l)>0. Furthermore, if ℐ=(t1,t2)\\mathcal{I}=(t\_{1},t\_{2}) with 0<t1<t20<t\_{1}<t\_{2} and a¯∈(0,inft∈ℐγ~​(t))\\underline{a}\\in(0,\\inf\_{t\\in\\mathcal{I}}\\tilde{\\gamma}(t)), then inft∈ℐ∂lW~T​(t,a¯)≥k>0\\inf\_{t\\in\\mathcal{I}}\\partial\_{l}\\tilde{W}\_{T}(t,\\underline{a})\\geq k>0 uniformly in T∈(T1,∞)T\\in(T\_{1},\\infty) for some T1>t2T\_{1}>t\_{2}.\
\

\

|     |     |     |     |     |\

| (6.32) |  | ∂lW~T​(t,l)\\displaystyle\\partial\_{l}\\tilde{W}\_{T}(t,l) | =𝔼⁡\[∫0ρ∂lLul​∂l(ℒ~​g~)​(Lul)​du\]+𝔼⁡\[∂lLρl​∂lW~T​(t+ρ,Lρl)\]\\displaystyle=\\mathbb{E}\\left\[\\int\_{0}^{\\rho}\\partial\_{l}L^{l}\_{u}\\partial\_{l}(\\tilde{\\mathcal{L}}\\tilde{g})(L^{l}\_{u})\\,du\\right\]+\\mathbb{E}\\left\[\\partial\_{l}L^{l}\_{\\rho}\\partial\_{l}\\tilde{W}\_{T}(t+\\rho,L^{l}\_{\\rho})\\right\] |  |\

\
and k>0k>0. Since T>t2T>t\_{2} was arbitrary, we arrive at the result.\
∎\
\
###### Remark 6.16.\
\
For each fixed TT, a positive lower bound on ∂lW~T​(t,a¯)\\partial\_{l}\\tilde{W}\_{T}(t,\\underline{a}) over a compact time interval would follow directly from pointwise positivity and continuity of (t,l)↦∂lW~T​(t,l)(t,l)\\mapsto\\partial\_{l}\\tilde{W}\_{T}(t,l). The argument above is needed\
to obtain a lower bound that is uniform in the horizon TT.\
\
In Proposition [6.13](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem13 "Proposition 6.13. ‣ 6.2. Properties of the optimal stopping boundary ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales") we obtained γ⁡(t)≤bT​(t)≤1\\gamma(t)\\leq b\_{T}(t)\\leq 1. To make the first inequality strict we will use the smooth-fit principle. To this end, we need sufficient regularity of the optimal stopping boundary. In the case of t↦c⁡(t)t\\mapsto c(t) increasing it is easy to show that t↦bT​(t)t\\mapsto b\_{T}(t) is decreasing, and so starting the process XxX^{x} at the boundary will result in the process hitting the interior of the stopping region immediately. However, since t↦c⁡(t)t\\mapsto c(t) is not assumed to be increasing, t↦bT​(t)t\\mapsto b\_{T}(t) may be increasing. We need to show that t↦bT​(t)t\\mapsto b\_{T}(t) does not increase too fast. To this end, we will show that bTb\_{T} is locally Lipschitz continuous by a method similar to \[ [12](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#bib.bib12 "")\].\
\
On the other hand, we can easily get bT​(t)<1b\_{T}(t)<1 on finite horizon by arguing as in ( [6.10](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E10 "In 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales"))–( [6.11](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E11 "In 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")). However, this argument does not work for infinite horizon if Assumption [B.(iii).(b)](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S3.I2.i3.I1.i2 "In B.(iii) ‣ Assumption B (Extended assumptions). ‣ 3.2. Assumptions ‣ 3. Setup and Preliminary Analysis ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales") is not satisfied. In addition, bTb\_{T} is lower semicontinuous, and thus we may get arbitrarily close to 11.\
To find a uniform upper bound away from 11 on any compact interval we will utilize a bound obtained from a particularly nice infinite-horizon problem in Lemma [6.17](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem17 "Lemma 6.17. ‣ 6.2. Properties of the optimal stopping boundary ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales").\
\
###### Lemma 6.17.\
\
For any ℐ=(t1,t2)\\mathcal{I}=(t\_{1},t\_{2}) with 0≤t1<t20\\leq t\_{1}<t\_{2}, there exists a constant a<1a<1 such that supt∈ℐbT​(t)≤a\\sup\_{t\\in\\mathcal{I}}b\_{T}(t)\\leq a uniformly in T∈(t2,∞\]T\\in(t\_{2},\\infty\].\

###### Proof.\
\
If Assumption [B.(iii).(b)](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S3.I2.i3.I1.i2 "In B.(iii) ‣ Assumption B (Extended assumptions). ‣ 3.2. Assumptions ‣ 3. Then c¯∈C1​(\[0,∞)),c¯​(t)>0\\underline{c}\\in C^{1}(\[0,\\infty)),\\underline{c}(t)>0, and\

\
so c¯\\underline{c} is decreasing. Hence, we can consider the infinite-horizon optimal stopping problem with running cost c¯\\underline{c} and value function V¯\\underline{V}.\

Take t∈(0,∞)t\\in(0,\\infty) and ε>0\\varepsilon>0 such that t≥εt\\geq\\varepsilon. Then\

\

\
Assume for contradiction that there exists a point t′≥0t^{\\prime}\\geq 0 such that b¯​(t′)=1\\underline{b}(t^{\\prime})=1. |     |     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- | --- |\
|  | \|∂taTδ​(t)\|\\displaystyle\\lvert\\partial\_{t}a\_{T}^{\\delta}(t)\\rvert | ≤K1K2​𝔼\[τ~∗∧τr+𝟏{τ~∗>τr}\]𝔼⁡\[τ~∗∧τr+𝟏{τ~∗>τr,τr<t2−t}\]≤K1K2​(1+ℙ⁡(τ~∗>τr)𝔼⁡\[τ~∗∧τr+𝟏{τ~∗>τr,τr<t2−t}\]).\\displaystyle\\leq\\frac{K\_{1}}{K\_{2}}\\frac{\\mathbb{E}\[\\tilde{\\tau}^{\*}\\wedge\\tau\_{r}+\\mathbf{1}\_{\\{\\tilde{\\tau}^{\*}>\\tau\_{r}\\}}\]}{\\mathbb{E}\[\\tilde{\\tau}^{\*}\\wedge\\tau\_{r}+\\mathbf{1}\_{\\{\\tilde{\\tau}^{\*}>\\tau\_{r},\\tau\_{r}<t\_{2}-t\\}}\]}\\leq\\frac{K\_{1}}{K\_{2}}\\left(1+\\frac{\\mathbb{P}(\\tilde{\\tau}^{\*}>\\tau\_{r})}{\\mathbb{E}\[\\tilde{\\tau}^{\*}\\wedge\\tau\_{r}+\\mathbf{1}\_{\\{\\tilde{\\tau}^{\*}>\\tau\_{r},\\tau\_{r}<t\_{2}-t\\}}\]}\\right). |  |\
\
Splitting the numerator into the space and time component of τr\\tau\_{r}, we note\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | ℙ⁡(τ~∗>τr,τr<t2−t)𝔼⁡\[τ~∗∧τr+𝟏{τ~∗>τr,τr<t2−t}\]≤1\\frac{\\mathbb{P}(\\tilde{\\tau}^{\*}>\\tau\_{r},\\tau\_{r}<t\_{2}-t)}{\\mathbb{E}\[\\tilde{\\tau}^{\*}\\wedge\\tau\_{r}+\\mathbf{1}\_{\\{\\tilde{\\tau}^{\*}>\\tau\_{r},\\tau\_{r}<t\_{2}-t\\}}\]}\\leq 1 |  |\
\
and\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | ℙ⁡(τ~∗>τr,τr=t2−t)𝔼⁡\[τ~∗∧τr+𝟏{τ~∗>τr,τr<t2−t}\]≤ℙ⁡(τ~∗>τr,τr=t2−t)𝔼⁡\[𝟏{τ~∗>τr,τr=t2−t}​(t2−t)\]≤ε−1\\frac{\\mathbb{P}(\\tilde{\\tau}^{\*}>\\tau\_{r},\\tau\_{r}=t\_{2}-t)}{\\mathbb{E}\[\\tilde{\\tau}^{\*}\\wedge\\tau\_{r}+\\mathbf{1}\_{\\{\\tilde{\\tau}^{\*}>\\tau\_{r},\\tau\_{r}<t\_{2}-t\\}}\]}\\leq\\frac{\\mathbb{P}(\\tilde{\\tau}^{\*}>\\tau\_{r},\\tau\_{r}=t\_{2}-t)}{\\mathbb{E}\[\\mathbf{1}\_{\\{\\tilde{\\tau}^{\*}>\\tau\_{r},\\tau\_{r}=t\_{2}-t\\}}(t\_{2}-t)\]}\\leq\\varepsilon^{-1} |  |\
\
since t∈ℐεt\\in\\mathcal{I}\_{\\varepsilon}.\
Hence, we have that ∂taTδ​(t)\\partial\_{t}a\_{T}^{\\delta}(t) is bounded for t∈ℐεt\\in\\mathcal{I}\_{\\varepsilon} by some KεK\_{\\varepsilon} that is uniform for (t,l)∈ℐε×𝒥(t,l)\\in\\mathcal{I}\_{\\varepsilon}\\times\\mathcal{J} and eventually uniformly in δ\\delta.\
∎\
\
###### Proposition 6.19.\
\
The optimal stopping boundary t↦bT​(t)t\\mapsto b\_{T}(t) is Liploc​(\[0,T))\\mathrm{Lip}\_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\[0,T)) for T≤∞T\\leq\\infty.\
\
###### Proof.\
\
First, let T<∞T<\\infty. As noted, we have that aTδ​(t)<b~T​(t)a\_{T}^{\\delta}(t)<\\tilde{b}\_{T}(t) for all t∈ℐt\\in\\mathcal{I}. Furthermore, since l↦W~T​(t,l)l\\mapsto\\tilde{W}\_{T}(t,l) is increasing, (aTδ)δ>0(a\_{T}^{\\delta})\_{\\delta>0} is increasing on t∈ℐt\\in\\mathcal{I} as δ↘0\\delta\\searrow 0. Hence, by monotone convergence, a limit aT0a\_{T}^{0} exists and satisfies aT0​(t)≤b~T​(t)a\_{T}^{0}(t)\\leq\\tilde{b}\_{T}(t). Since aTδa\_{T}^{\\delta} are the level sets and W~T\\tilde{W}\_{T} is continuous, we get W~T​(t,aT0​(t))=0\\tilde{W}\_{T}(t,a\_{T}^{0}(t))=0, and hence aT0​(t)≥b~T​(t)a\_{T}^{0}(t)\\geq\\tilde{b}\_{T}(t). Thus, we have aTδ​(t)→b~T​(t)a\_{T}^{\\delta}(t)\\to\\tilde{b}\_{T}(t) for t∈ℐt\\in\\mathcal{I}. To conclude local Lipschitz continuity we use the pointwise convergence and Lemma [6.18](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem18 "Lemma 6.18. ‣ 6.2. Properties of the optimal stopping boundary ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales"). That is,\
for t,s∈ℐεt,s\\in\\mathcal{I}\_{\\varepsilon} we can write\
\
|     |     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- | --- |\
|  | \|b~T​(t)−b~T​(s)\|≤\\displaystyle\\lvert\\tilde{b}\_{T}(t)-\\tilde{b}\_{T}(s)\\rvert\\leq | \|b~T​(t)−aTδ​(t)\|+\|aTδ​(t)−aTδ​(s)\|+\|aTδ​(s)−b~T​(s)\|\\displaystyle\\lvert\\tilde{b}\_{T}(t)-a\_{T}^{\\delta}(t)\\rvert+\\lvert a\_{T}^{\\delta}(t)-a\_{T}^{\\delta}(s)\\rvert+\\lvert a\_{T}^{\\delta}(s)-\\tilde{b}\_{T}(s)\\rvert |  |\
|  | ≤\\displaystyle\\leq | \|b~T​(t)−aTδ​(t)\|+Kε​\|t−s\|+\|aTδ​(s)−b~T​(s)\|\\displaystyle\\lvert\\tilde{b}\_{T}(t)-a\_{T}^{\\delta}(t)\\rvert+K\_{\\varepsilon}\\lvert t-s\\rvert+\\lvert a\_{T}^{\\delta}(s)-\\tilde{b}\_{T}(s)\\rvert |  |\
|  |  | →Kε​\|t−s\|\\displaystyle\\to K\_{\\varepsilon}\\lvert t-s\\rvert |  |\
\
as δ→0\\delta\\to 0, and hence b~T∈Liploc​((0,T))\\tilde{b}\_{T}\\in\\mathrm{Lip}\_{\\mathrm{loc}}((0,T)).\
For infinite horizon, note that\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | \|b~∞​(t)−b~∞​(s)\|≤\|b~∞​(t)−b~T​(t)\|+Kε​\|t−s\|+\|b~∞​(s)−b~T​(s)\|→Kε​\|t−s\|,\\lvert\\tilde{b}\_{\\infty}(t)-\\tilde{b}\_{\\infty}(s)\\rvert\\leq\\lvert\\tilde{b}\_{\\infty}(t)-\\tilde{b}\_{T}(t)\\rvert+K\_{\\varepsilon}\\lvert t-s\\rvert+\\lvert\\tilde{b}\_{\\infty}(s)-\\tilde{b}\_{T}(s)\\rvert\\to K\_{\\varepsilon}\\lvert t-s\\rvert, |  |\
\
as T→∞T\\to\\infty by ( [6.31](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E31 "In Proof. ‣ 6.2. Properties of the optimal stopping boundary ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")).\
Finally, we can also conclude that bTb\_{T} is locally Lipschitz on \[0,T)\[0,T) since Ψ−1\\Psi^{-1} is Lipschitz on 𝒥\\mathcal{J} and the extension to 00 is due to ( [6.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E1 "In 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")).\
∎\
\
### 6.3. Regularity and the smooth fit\
\
Next, we use the Lipschitz boundary to show that the stopping set is probabilistically regular, which in turn gives the smooth-fit property of the value function in Theorem [4.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S4.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 4.2 (Regularity and smooth fit). ‣ 4. Main Results ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales") for finite horizon due to \[ [11](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#bib.bib11 "")\].\
\
###### Lemma 6.20.\
\
Let ((tn,xn))n∈ℕ⊂𝒞T((t\_{n},x\_{n}))\_{n\\in\\mathbb{N}}\\subset\\mathcal{C}\_{T} with (tn,xn)→(t,bT​(t))(t\_{n},x\_{n})\\to(t,b\_{T}(t)). Then τT∗​(tn,xn)→0\\tau^{\*}\_{T}(t\_{n},x\_{n})\\to 0 almost surely for any T≤∞T\\leq\\infty.\
\
###### Proof.\
\
Let t∈(0,T)t\\in(0,T), take ε>0\\varepsilon>0 and t1,t2∈(0,T)t\_{1},t\_{2}\\in(0,T) such that tt is in the interior of ℐε=\[t1+ε,t2−ε\]\\mathcal{I}\_{\\varepsilon}=\[t\_{1}+\\varepsilon,t\_{2}-\\varepsilon\] as above, and let KεK\_{\\varepsilon} be the upper Lipschitz constant for b~T\\tilde{b}\_{T} on ℐε\\mathcal{I}\_{\\varepsilon}. Set l=b~T​(t)l=\\tilde{b}\_{T}(t) and take a compact 𝒥:=\[l−r,l+r\]⊂Ψ⁡((0,1))\\mathcal{J}:=\[l-r,l+r\]\\subset\\Psi((0,1)) for some r>0r>0. Define the stopping time\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | τ𝒥:=inf{s≥0:Lsl∉𝒥},\\tau^{\\mathcal{J}}:=\\inf\\{s\\geq 0:L^{l}\_{s}\\not\\in\\mathcal{J}\\}, |  |\
\
for which we note τ𝒥>0\\tau^{\\mathcal{J}}>0 almost surely since LlL^{l} has continuous paths. Now consider the stopped exponential martingale\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | Zs𝒥:=ℰ⁡(∫0s∧τ𝒥(Kε−μ⁡(Lul))​d​Bu).Z^{\\mathcal{J}}\_{s}:=\\mathcal{E}\\left(\\int\_{0}^{s\\wedge\\tau^{\\mathcal{J}}}(K\_{\\varepsilon}-\\mu(L^{l}\_{u}))\\,dB\_{u}\\right). |  |\
\
The process Z𝒥Z^{\\mathcal{J}} is indeed a true martingale on, say, s∈\[0,δ\]s\\in\[0,\\delta\] for δ:=t2−ε−t>0\\delta:=t\_{2}-\\varepsilon-t>0, by Novikov’s condition due to the localization on 𝒥\\mathcal{J}. By Girsanov’s theorem, we can define the new measure ℚ𝒥\\mathbb{Q}^{\\mathcal{J}} on ℱδ\\mathcal{F}\_{\\delta} by\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | d​ℚ𝒥d​ℙ\|ℱδ=Zδ𝒥,\\frac{d\\mathbb{Q}^{\\mathcal{J}}}{d\\mathbb{P}}\\Bigg\|\_{\\mathcal{F}\_{\\delta}}=Z^{\\mathcal{J}}\_{\\delta}, |  |\
\
and, up to τ𝒥\\tau^{\\mathcal{J}}, it holds that\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | Lsl−Kε​s=l+Bs𝒥,L^{l}\_{s}-K\_{\\varepsilon}s=l+B^{\\mathcal{J}}\_{s}, |  |\
\
where B𝒥B^{\\mathcal{J}} is a Brownian motion under ℚ𝒥\\mathbb{Q}^{\\mathcal{J}}.\
\
Consider the first hitting time to the upper boundary\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | σl:=inf{s>0:Lsl>b~T​(t+s)}.\\sigma^{l}:=\\inf\\{s>0:L^{l}\_{s}>\\tilde{b}\_{T}(t+s)\\}. |  |\
\
Since b~T\\tilde{b}\_{T} is Lipschitz, we have\
\
|     |     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- | --- |\
| (6.39) |  | σ¯l𝒥:=inf{0<s<δ∧τ𝒥:Lsl−Kε​s>l}≥σl\\overline{\\sigma}\_{l}^{\\mathcal{J}}:=\\inf\\{0<s<\\delta\\wedge\\tau^{\\mathcal{J}}:L^{l}\_{s}-K\_{\\varepsilon}s>l\\}\\geq\\sigma^{l} |  |\
\
where inf\\inf of the empty set is ∞\\infty by convention. Thus, by the previous considerations, we have\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | σ¯l𝒥=inf{0<s<δ∧τ𝒥:Bs𝒥>0}\\overline{\\sigma}\_{l}^{\\mathcal{J}}=\\inf\\{0<s<\\delta\\wedge\\tau^{\\mathcal{J}}:B^{\\mathcal{J}}\_{s}>0\\} |  |\
\
and, specifically, σ¯l𝒥=0\\overline{\\sigma}\_{l}^{\\mathcal{J}}=0 holds ℚ𝒥\\mathbb{Q}^{\\mathcal{J}}–almost surely by standard results, see e.g. \[ [23](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#bib.bib23 ""), Problem 7.18\].\
Since ℙ\\mathbb{P} is an equivalent measure, we have\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | ℙ⁡(σl=0)=1\\mathbb{P}(\\sigma^{l}=0)=1 |  |\
\
by ( [6.39](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E39 "In Proof. ‣ 6.3. Regularity and the smooth fit ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")).\
Thus l=b~T​(t)l=\\tilde{b}\_{T}(t) is probabilistically regular for (𝒟~T)∘(\\tilde{\\mathcal{D}}\_{T})^{\\circ}, and thus also 𝒟~T\\tilde{\\mathcal{D}}\_{T}, with respect to LL. Since tt was arbitrary, for any t∈(0,T)t\\in(0,T), the boundary point (t,l)=(t,b~T​(t))(t,l)=(t,\\tilde{b}\_{T}(t)) is probabilistically regular for 𝒟~T\\tilde{\\mathcal{D}}\_{T} and (𝒟~T)∘(\\tilde{\\mathcal{D}}\_{T})^{\\circ}. Furthermore,\
since l↦Lsll\\mapsto L^{l}\_{s} is continuous, it also holds that (t,b~T​(t))(t,\\tilde{b}\_{T}(t)) is Green regular for 𝒟~T\\tilde{\\mathcal{D}}\_{T} and (𝒟~T)∘(\\tilde{\\mathcal{D}}\_{T})^{\\circ} by \[ [11](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#bib.bib11 ""), Corollary 5\], that is,\
\
|     |     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- | --- |\
| (6.40) |  | lim𝒞~T∋(tn,ln)→(t,b~T​(t))ℙ⁡(τ⁡(tn,ln)≥δ)=0\\lim\_{\\tilde{\\mathcal{C}}\_{T}\\ni(t\_{n},l\_{n})\\to(t,\\tilde{b}\_{T}(t))}\\mathbb{P}(\\tau(t\_{n},l\_{n})\\geq\\delta)=0 |  |\
\
for every δ>0\\delta>0, where\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | τ⁡(t,l)=inf{s≥0:(t+s,Lsl)∈A}\\tau(t,l)=\\inf\\{s\\geq 0:(t+s,L^{l}\_{s})\\in A\\} |  |\
\
for A=𝒟~TA=\\tilde{\\mathcal{D}}\_{T} and A=(𝒟~T)∘A=(\\tilde{\\mathcal{D}}\_{T})^{\\circ}. In fact, \[ [11](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#bib.bib11 ""), Corollary 6\] upgrades the convergence in probability to almost sure convergence. The same conclusions can then be translated directly to the 𝒮T\\mathcal{S}\_{T} coordinates and extended to t=0t=0 by ( [6.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E1 "In 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")).\
\
Specifically, for ((tn,xn))n∈ℕ⊂𝒞T((t\_{n},x\_{n}))\_{n\\in\\mathbb{N}}\\subset\\mathcal{C}\_{T} with (tn,xn)→(t,bT​(t))(t\_{n},x\_{n})\\to(t,b\_{T}(t)) it holds that τT∗​(tn,xn)→0\\tau^{\*}\_{T}(t\_{n},x\_{n})\\to 0 almost surely.\
∎\
\
We can now prove the temporal smooth fit. We note that since VTV\_{T} is C1,2C^{1,2} on 𝒞T\\mathcal{C}\_{T}, the probabilistic derivative representations derived above hold at every point of 𝒞T\\mathcal{C}\_{T}, rather than merely almost everywhere.\
\
###### Proposition 6.21.\
\
For any T≤∞T\\leq\\infty, let ((tn,xn))n∈ℕ((t\_{n},x\_{n}))\_{n\\in\\mathbb{N}} be a sequence as in Lemma [6.20](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem20 "Lemma 6.20. ‣ 6.3. Regularity and the smooth fit ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales"). Then\
\
|     |     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- | --- |\
| (6.41) |  | limn→∞∂tVT​(tn,xn)=0.\\lim\_{n\\to\\infty}\\partial\_{t}V\_{T}(t\_{n},x\_{n})=0. |  |\
\
###### Proof.\
\
It holds that 𝔼⁡\[c⁡(tn+τT∗​(tn,xn))\]−c⁡(tn)→0\\mathbb{E}\[c(t\_{n}+\\tau^{\*}\_{T}(t\_{n},x\_{n}))\]-c(t\_{n})\\to 0 since cc is bounded and τT∗​(tn,xn)→0\\tau^{\*}\_{T}(t\_{n},x\_{n})\\to 0 almost surely. Furthermore, if T<∞T<\\infty, then, by ( [6.40](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E40 "In Proof. ‣ 6.3. Regularity and the smooth fit ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")), we have ℙ⁡(τT∗​(tn,xn)=T−tn)→0\\mathbb{P}(\\tau^{\*}\_{T}(t\_{n},x\_{n})=T-t\_{n})\\to 0. Hence ( [6.13](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E13 "In Proposition 6.6. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")) and ( [6.14](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E14 "In Proposition 6.6. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")) imply the temporal smooth fit.\
∎\
\
The spatial smooth fit requires more work in the infinite-horizon case. Indeed, we will need the finite-horizon smooth fit for the approach below.\
\
###### Proposition 6.22.\
\
For any T<∞T<\\infty, let ((tn,xn))n∈ℕ((t\_{n},x\_{n}))\_{n\\in\\mathbb{N}} be a sequence as in Lemma [6.20](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem20 "Lemma 6.20. ‣ 6.3. Regularity and the smooth fit ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales"). Then\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | limn→∞∂xVT​(tn,xn)=g′​(bT​(t)).\\lim\_{n\\to\\infty}\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}(t\_{n},x\_{n})=g^{\\prime}(b\_{T}(t)). |  |\
\
###### Proof.\
\
By an application of dominated convergence theorem and Scheffé’s lemma, since g′g^{\\prime} is locally bounded by KspaceK\_{\\text{space}}, and ∂xXx\\partial\_{x}X^{x} is a true martingale on \[0,T\]\[0,T\], as in Lemma [6.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem2 "Lemma 6.2. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales") and Proposition [6.4](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem4 "Proposition 6.4. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales"), we have\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | limn→∞∂xVT​(tn,xn)=limn→∞𝔼⁡\[g′​(XτT∗​(tn,xn)xn)​∂xXτT∗​(tn,xn)xn\]=g′​(bT​(t)),\\lim\_{n\\to\\infty}\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}(t\_{n},x\_{n})=\\lim\_{n\\to\\infty}\\mathbb{E}\\left\[g^{\\prime}(X^{x\_{n}}\_{\\tau^{\*}\_{T}(t\_{n},x\_{n})})\\partial\_{x}X^{x\_{n}}\_{\\tau^{\*}\_{T}(t\_{n},x\_{n})}\\right\]=g^{\\prime}(b\_{T}(t)), |  |\
\
using τT∗​(tn,xn)→0\\tau^{\*}\_{T}(t\_{n},x\_{n})\\to 0 almost surely.\
∎\
\
In the proof of the spatial smooth fit we made use of the probabilistic representation of the spatial derivative on finite horizon. This derivative is not directly available in the infinite-horizon case. We thus must take a different approach. We first prove the following convergence result.\
\
###### Proposition 6.23.\
\
For every ℐ=\[t1,t2\]⊂\[0,∞)\\mathcal{I}=\[t\_{1},t\_{2}\]\\subset\[0,\\infty) and 𝒥=\[a¯,a¯\]⊂(0,1)\\mathcal{J}=\[\\underline{a},\\overline{a}\]\\subset(0,1) it holds that x↦V∞​(t,x)x\\mapsto V\_{\\infty}(t,x) is differentiable, and\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | ∂xVT→∂xV∞\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}\\to\\partial\_{x}V\_{\\infty} |  |\
\
uniformly on ℐ×𝒥\\mathcal{I}\\times\\mathcal{J} for T→∞T\\to\\infty.\
\
###### Proof.\
\
Let δ>0\\delta>0 such that 𝒥δ=\[a¯−δ,a¯+δ\]⊂(0,1)\\mathcal{J}\_{\\delta}=\[\\underline{a}-\\delta,\\overline{a}+\\delta\]\\subset(0,1).\
We will first show that ∂x​xVT\\partial\_{xx}V\_{T} is uniformly bounded on ℐ×𝒥δ\\mathcal{I}\\times\\mathcal{J}\_{\\delta} to show that x↦∂xVT​(t,x)x\\mapsto\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}(t,x) is Lipschitz continuous.\
\
Recall from Corollary [6.12](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem12 "Corollary 6.12. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales") that we have an expression for ∂x​xVT​(t,x)\\partial\_{xx}V\_{T}(t,x), which can be bounded by\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | \|∂x​xVT​(t,x)\|≤\|g′′​(x)\|+2​c⁡(t)+\|∂tVT​(t,x)\|σ2​(x).\|\\partial\_{xx}V\_{T}(t,x)\|\\leq\|g^{\\prime\\prime}(x)\|+2\\frac{c(t)+\|\\partial\_{t}V\_{T}(t,x)\|}{\\sigma^{2}(x)}. |  |\
\
It holds that supx∈𝒥δ\|g′′​(x)\|<∞\\sup\_{x\\in\\mathcal{J}\_{\\delta}}\|g^{\\prime\\prime}(x)\|<\\infty, infx∈𝒥δσ2​(x)>0\\inf\_{x\\in\\mathcal{J}\_{\\delta}}\\sigma^{2}(x)>0, and supt∈ℐc⁡(t)<∞\\sup\_{t\\in\\mathcal{I}}c(t)<\\infty. Furthermore, \|∂tVT​(t,x)\|≤Ktime\|\\partial\_{t}V\_{T}(t,x)\|\\leq K\_{\\text{time}} from Proposition [6.6](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem6 "Proposition 6.6. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales") which is independent of TT. Thus, there exists a uniform constant KδK\_{\\delta} such that\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | \|∂x​xVT​(t,x)\|≤Kδ,\|\\partial\_{xx}V\_{T}(t,x)\|\\leq K\_{\\delta}, |  |\
\
whenever it exists on ℐ×𝒥δ\\mathcal{I}\\times\\mathcal{J}\_{\\delta}.\
It then follows that\
\
|     |     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- | --- |\
| (6.42) |  | \|∂xVT​(t,x)−∂xVT​(t,y)\|≤Kδ​\|x−y\|,\|\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}(t,x)-\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}(t,y)\|\\leq K\_{\\delta}\|x-y\|, |  |\
\
for all x,y∈𝒥δx,y\\in\\mathcal{J}\_{\\delta}. Namely, we may, without loss of generality, assume x<bT​(t)<yx<b\_{T}(t)<y. Then, by smooth fit, we have\
\
|     |     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- | --- |\
|  | \|∂xVT​(t,x)−∂xVT​(t,y)\|=\\displaystyle\|\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}(t,x)-\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}(t,y)\|= | \|∂xVT​(t,x)−∂xVT​(t,bT​(t)−)+∂xVT​(t,bT​(t)+)−∂xVT​(t,y)\|\\displaystyle\|\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}(t,x)-\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}(t,b\_{T}(t)-)+\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}(t,b\_{T}(t)+)-\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}(t,y)\| |  |\
|  | =\\displaystyle= | \|∫xbT​(t)∂x​xVT​(t,z)​𝑑z+∫bT​(t)y∂x​xVT​(t,z)​𝑑z\|≤Kδ​\|x−y\|.\\displaystyle\\left\|\\int\_{x}^{b\_{T}(t)}\\partial\_{xx}V\_{T}(t,z)\\,dz+\\int\_{b\_{T}(t)}^{y}\\partial\_{xx}V\_{T}(t,z)\\,dz\\right\|\\leq K\_{\\delta}\|x-y\|. |  |\
\
Next, we show that ∂xVT\\partial\_{x}V\_{T} is uniformly Cauchy in TT on ℐ×𝒥\\mathcal{I}\\times\\mathcal{J}. Take h∈(0,δ)h\\in(0,\\delta) and x∈𝒥x\\in\\mathcal{J} such that x+h∈𝒥δx+h\\in\\mathcal{J}\_{\\delta}. By the fundamental theorem of calculus\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | VT​(t,x+h)−VT​(t,x)=∫0h∂xVT​(t,x+r)​𝑑r,V\_{T}(t,x+h)-V\_{T}(t,x)=\\int\_{0}^{h}\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}(t,x+r)dr, |  |\
\
and so, by the triangle inequality,\
\
|     |     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- | --- |\
| (6.43) |  | \|VT​(t,x+h)−VT​(t,x)h−∂xVT​(t,x)\|≤1h​∫0h\|∂xVT​(t,x+r)−∂xVT​(t,x)\|​𝑑r≤h​Kδ2\\left\|\\frac{V\_{T}(t,x+h)-V\_{T}(t,x)}{h}-\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}(t,x)\\right\|\\leq\\frac{1}{h}\\int\_{0}^{h}\|\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}(t,x+r)-\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}(t,x)\|\\,dr\\leq\\frac{hK\_{\\delta}}{2} |  |\
\
by ( [6.42](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E42 "In Proof. ‣ 6.3. Regularity and the smooth fit ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")). For T,S>t2T,S>t\_{2}, we then have\
\
|     |     |     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\
| (6.44) |  |  | \|∂xVT​(t,x)−∂xVS​(t,x)\|\\displaystyle\|\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}(t,x)-\\partial\_{x}V\_{S}(t,x)\| |  |\
| (6.45) |  | ≤\\displaystyle\\leq | \|∂xVT​(t,x)−VT​(t,x+h)−VT​(t,x)h\|+\|VT​(t,x+h)−VS​(t,x+h)h\|\\displaystyle\\left\|\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}(t,x)-\\frac{V\_{T}(t,x+h)-V\_{T}(t,x)}{h}\\right\|+\\left\|\\frac{V\_{T}(t,x+h)-V\_{S}(t,x+h)}{h}\\right\| |  |\
| (6.46) |  |  | +\|VS​(t,x)−VT​(t,x)h\|+\|VS​(t,x+h)−VS​(t,x)h−∂xVS​(t,x)\|\\displaystyle+\\left\|\\frac{V\_{S}(t,x)-V\_{T}(t,x)}{h}\\right\|+\\left\|\\frac{V\_{S}(t,x+h)-V\_{S}(t,x)}{h}-\\partial\_{x}V\_{S}(t,x)\\right\| |  |\
| (6.47) |  | ≤\\displaystyle\\leq | h​Kδ+2h​‖VT−VS‖L∞​(ℐ×𝒥δ),\\displaystyle hK\_{\\delta}+\\frac{2}{h}\\\|V\_{T}-V\_{S}\\\|\_{L^{\\infty}(\\mathcal{I}\\times\\mathcal{J}\_{\\delta})}, |  |\
\
by ( [6.43](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E43 "In Proof. ‣ 6.3. Regularity and the smooth fit ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")). Since VTV\_{T} converges uniformly on ℐ×𝒥δ\\mathcal{I}\\times\\mathcal{J}\_{\\delta} as T→∞T\\to\\infty, it specifically holds that VTV\_{T} is uniformly Cauchy, hence, for any ε>0\\varepsilon>0, we can choose T,ST,S large enough such that\
‖VT−VS‖L∞​(ℐ×𝒥δ)<h​ε/4\\\|V\_{T}-V\_{S}\\\|\_{L^{\\infty}(\\mathcal{I}\\times\\mathcal{J}\_{\\delta})}<h\\varepsilon/4. Similarly, we can take h<ε/(2​Kδ)h<\\varepsilon/(2K\_{\\delta}). Thus, taking supremum over ℐ×𝒥\\mathcal{I}\\times\\mathcal{J} in ( [6.44](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E44 "In Proof. ‣ 6.3. Regularity and the smooth fit ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales"))-( [6.47](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E47 "In Proof. ‣ 6.3. Regularity and the smooth fit ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")), we get\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | ‖∂xVT−∂xVS‖L∞​(ℐ×𝒥)<ε,\\\|\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}-\\partial\_{x}V\_{S}\\\|\_{L^{\\infty}(\\mathcal{I}\\times\\mathcal{J})}<\\varepsilon, |  |\
\
i.e. ∂xVT\\partial\_{x}V\_{T} is uniformly Cauchy in TT on ℐ×𝒥\\mathcal{I}\\times\\mathcal{J} and so it converges uniformly to some function (t,x)↦U⁡(t,x)(t,x)\\mapsto U(t,x). By the fundamental theorem of calculus, we have\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | V∞​(t,x)−V∞​(t,y)=limT→∞∫yx∂xVT​(t,z)​𝑑z=∫yxU⁡(t,z)​𝑑z,V\_{\\infty}(t,x)-V\_{\\infty}(t,y)=\\lim\_{T\\to\\infty}\\int\_{y}^{x}\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}(t,z)\\,dz=\\int\_{y}^{x}U(t,z)\\,dz, |  |\
\
so we identify U⁡(t,x)=∂xV∞​(t,x)U(t,x)=\\partial\_{x}V\_{\\infty}(t,x).\
∎\
\
Using this, we can now proceed to the proof of the main result, Theorem [4.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S4.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 4.2 (Regularity and smooth fit). ‣ 4. Main Results ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales").\
\
###### Proof of Theorem [4.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1\#S4.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 4.2 (Regularity and smooth fit). ‣ 4. Main Results ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales").\
\
Since VTV\_{T} is locally Lipschitz and equals gg on 𝒟T\\mathcal{D}\_{T}, once\
∂tVT\\partial\_{t}V\_{T} and ∂xVT\\partial\_{x}V\_{T} on 𝒞T\\mathcal{C}\_{T} extend continuously\
to the boundary with limits 00 and g′g^{\\prime}, respectively, a standard pasting argument yields that VT∈C1​(𝒮T)V\_{T}\\in C^{1}(\\mathcal{S}\_{T}).\
By this observation it suffices to restrict to sequences approaching the boundary from the continuation region. By Propositions [6.11](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem11 "Proposition 6.11. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales"), [6.21](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem21 "Proposition 6.21. ‣ 6.3. Regularity and the smooth fit ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales"), and\
[6.22](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem22 "Proposition 6.22. ‣ 6.3. Regularity and the smooth fit ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales"), we are only missing the spatial smooth fit\
for the infinite-horizon case.\
\
Let ((tn,xn))n∈ℕ((t\_{n},x\_{n}))\_{n\\in\\mathbb{N}} be as in Lemma [6.20](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem20 "Lemma 6.20. ‣ 6.3. Regularity and the smooth fit ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales") (for T=∞T=\\infty).\
Since b∞b\_{\\infty} is locally Lipschitz by Proposition\
[6.19](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem19 "Proposition 6.19. ‣ 6.2. Properties of the optimal stopping boundary ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales"), b∞​(tn)→b∞​(t)b\_{\\infty}(t\_{n})\\to b\_{\\infty}(t) and \|xn−b∞​(tn)\|→0\|x\_{n}-b\_{\\infty}(t\_{n})\|\\to 0.\
By restricting to sufficiently large nn, all the points\
(tn,xn)(t\_{n},x\_{n}) and (tn,b∞​(tn))(t\_{n},b\_{\\infty}(t\_{n})) lie in a common compact subset of \[0,∞)×(0,1)\[0,\\infty)\\times(0,1).\
\
Using the inequality\
\
|     |     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- | --- |\
|  | \|∂xV∞​(tn,xn)−∂xV∞​(t,b∞​(t))\|\\displaystyle\\left\|\\partial\_{x}V\_{\\infty}(t\_{n},x\_{n})-\\partial\_{x}V\_{\\infty}(t,b\_{\\infty}(t))\\right\| | ≤\|∂xV∞​(tn,xn)−∂xV∞​(tn,b∞​(tn))\|\\displaystyle\\leq\\left\|\\partial\_{x}V\_{\\infty}(t\_{n},x\_{n})-\\partial\_{x}V\_{\\infty}(t\_{n},b\_{\\infty}(t\_{n}))\\right\| |  |\
|  |  | +\|∂xV∞​(tn,b∞​(tn))−∂xV∞​(t,b∞​(t))\|,\\displaystyle\\quad+\\left\|\\partial\_{x}V\_{\\infty}(t\_{n},b\_{\\infty}(t\_{n}))-\\partial\_{x}V\_{\\infty}(t,b\_{\\infty}(t))\\right\|, |  |\
\
we will establish that each term vanishes as n→∞n\\to\\infty.\
By Proposition [6.23](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem23 "Proposition 6.23. ‣ 6.3. Regularity and the smooth fit ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales") and the estimate\
( [6.42](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E42 "In Proof. ‣ 6.3. Regularity and the smooth fit ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")) (which is uniform in the horizon TT), there is a constant KK depending on the compact such that\
\
|     |     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- | --- |\
|  | \|∂xV∞​(tn,xn)−∂xV∞​(tn,b∞​(tn))\|\\displaystyle\\left\|\\partial\_{x}V\_{\\infty}(t\_{n},x\_{n})-\\partial\_{x}V\_{\\infty}(t\_{n},b\_{\\infty}(t\_{n}))\\right\| | =limT→∞\|∂xVT​(tn,xn)−∂xVT​(tn,b∞​(tn))\|\\displaystyle=\\lim\_{T\\to\\infty}\\left\|\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}(t\_{n},x\_{n})-\\partial\_{x}V\_{T}(t\_{n},b\_{\\infty}(t\_{n}))\\right\| |  |\
|  |  | ≤K​\|xn−b∞​(tn)\|→0.\\displaystyle\\quad\\leq K\|x\_{n}-b\_{\\infty}(t\_{n})\|\\to 0. |  |\
\
At the same time, Proposition [6.23](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem23 "Proposition 6.23. ‣ 6.3. Regularity and the smooth fit ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales") implies that\
x↦V∞​(s,x)x\\mapsto V\_{\\infty}(s,x) is differentiable on (0,1)(0,1) for every\
s≥0s\\geq 0. Since V∞​(s,y)=g⁡(y)V\_{\\infty}(s,y)=g(y), y≥b∞​(s)y\\geq b\_{\\infty}(s), differentiability at the boundary gives ∂xV∞​(s,b∞​(s))=g′​(b∞​(s))\\partial\_{x}V\_{\\infty}(s,b\_{\\infty}(s))=g^{\\prime}(b\_{\\infty}(s)), s≥0s\\geq 0. Consequently,\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | \|∂xV∞​(tn,b∞​(tn))−∂xV∞​(t,b∞​(t))\|=\|g′​(b∞​(tn))−g′​(b∞​(t))\|→0\\displaystyle\\left\|\\partial\_{x}V\_{\\infty}(t\_{n},b\_{\\infty}(t\_{n}))-\\partial\_{x}V\_{\\infty}(t,b\_{\\infty}(t))\\right\|=\\left\|g^{\\prime}(b\_{\\infty}(t\_{n}))-g^{\\prime}(b\_{\\infty}(t))\\right\|\\to 0 |  |\
\
by continuity of b∞b\_{\\infty} and g′g^{\\prime}.\
This completes the proof for the upper boundary, while the lower boundary holds similarly by symmetry. Hence we are done.\
∎\
\
With the smooth-fit property of the value function in finite horizon we are finally able to verify that the optimal stopping boundary is away from the set 𝒰\\mathcal{U}. This in turn will give us the final part of Theorem [4.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S4.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem 4.1 (Structure of the continuation region). ‣ 4. Main Results ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales").\
\
###### Proof of Theorem [4.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1\#S4.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem 4.1 (Structure of the continuation region). ‣ 4. Main Results ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales").\
\
The optimality of the first hitting time to the stopping set was proven in Proposition [3.7](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S3.Thmtheorem7 "Proposition 3.7. ‣ 3.5. Preliminary Analysis ‣ 3. Setup and Preliminary Analysis ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales") for finite horizon and Proposition [6.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem1 "Proposition 6.1. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales") for infinite horizon.\
The existence of a lower semicontinuous boundary bTb\_{T} such that bT​(t)≥γ⁡(t)b\_{T}(t)\\geq\\gamma(t) and the continuation set is given by ( [6.30](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E30 "In Proposition 6.13. ‣ 6.2. Properties of the optimal stopping boundary ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")) was proven in Proposition [6.13](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem13 "Proposition 6.13. ‣ 6.2. Properties of the optimal stopping boundary ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales").\
From Lemma [6.17](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem17 "Lemma 6.17. ‣ 6.2. Properties of the optimal stopping boundary ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales"), we have bT​(t)<1b\_{T}(t)<1, and so we are only missing bT​(t)>γ​(t)b\_{T}(t)>\\gamma(t) for all t∈\[0,T)t\\in\[0,T) with T≤∞T\\leq\\infty.\
\
If the conclusion holds for finite TT, then it can be extended to infinite horizon by noting that T↦bTT\\mapsto b\_{T} is increasing. Let T<∞T<\\infty and assume for contradiction that bT​(t0)=γ⁡(t0)b\_{T}(t\_{0})=\\gamma(t\_{0}) for some t0∈(0,T)t\_{0}\\in(0,T). Choose δ,η>0\\delta,\\eta>0, with t0+δ<Tt\_{0}+\\delta<T, such that (t,γ⁡(t)+z)∈𝒰(t,\\gamma(t)+z)\\in\\mathcal{U} whenever \|t−t0\|<δ\|t-t\_{0}\|<\\delta and z∈(−η,0)z\\in(-\\eta,0). Set\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | W¯​(t,z):=WT​(t,γ⁡(t)+z).\\overline{W}(t,z):=W\_{T}(t,\\gamma(t)+z). |  |\
\
Then W¯<0\\overline{W}<0 in this cylinder and W¯​(t0,0)=0\\overline{W}(t\_{0},0)=0. Moreover,\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | ∂tW¯−γ′​(t)​∂zW¯+12​σ2​(γ⁡(t)+z)​∂z​zW¯=−(c⁡(t)+ℒ​g​(γ⁡(t)+z))>0.\\partial\_{t}\\overline{W}-\\gamma^{\\prime}(t)\\partial\_{z}\\overline{W}+\\frac{1}{2}\\sigma^{2}(\\gamma(t)+z)\\partial\_{zz}\\overline{W}=-\\bigl(c(t)+\\mathcal{L}g(\\gamma(t)+z)\\bigr)>0. |  |\
\
The operator is uniformly parabolic on the closure of a smaller cylinder. After reversing time, the parabolic Hopf lemma, see e.g. \[ [29](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#bib.bib29 ""), Theorem 3.2.3\], at the flat lateral boundary z=0z=0 gives\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | ∂zW¯​(t0,0)>0.\\partial\_{z}\\overline{W}(t\_{0},0)>0. |  |\
\
On the other hand, spatial smooth fit gives\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | ∂zW¯​(t0,0)=∂xWT​(t0,γ⁡(t0))=0,\\partial\_{z}\\overline{W}(t\_{0},0)=\\partial\_{x}W\_{T}(t\_{0},\\gamma(t\_{0}))=0, |  |\
\
which is a contradiction. Hence bT​(t)>γ​(t)b\_{T}(t)>\\gamma(t) for t∈(0,T)t\\in(0,T).\
The conclusion can be extended to t∈\[0,T)t\\in\[0,T) by arguing as around ( [6.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E1 "In 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")).\
∎\
\
The separation of the optimal stopping boundary from 𝒰\\mathcal{U} is useful since it yields a strict inequality c⁡(t)+ℒ​g​(x)>0c(t)+\\mathcal{L}g(x)>0 close to the boundary. First, this can be utilized to conclude that the smooth fit is in some sense the maximal regularity we can achieve as stated in Corollary [4.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S4.Thmtheorem3 "Corollary 4.3. ‣ 4. Main Results ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales").\
\
###### Proof of Corollary [4.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1\#S4.Thmtheorem3 "Corollary 4.3. ‣ 4. Main Results ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales").\
\
The first part is given by Corollary [6.12](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem12 "Corollary 6.12. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales").\
For the second part, Proposition [6.11](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.Thmtheorem11 "Proposition 6.11. ‣ 6.1. Properties of the value function ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales") yields that ∂x​xVT\\partial\_{xx}V\_{T} is continuous in 𝒞T\\mathcal{C}\_{T}. We can utilize ( [6.41](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S6.E41 "In Proposition 6.21. ‣ 6.3. Regularity and the smooth fit ‣ 6. Inhomogeneous Problems ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales")) to get\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | limn→∞∂x​xVT​(tn,xn)=−2​c​(t)σ2​(bT​(t)),\\lim\_{n\\to\\infty}\\partial\_{xx}V\_{T}(t\_{n},x\_{n})=-\\frac{2c(t)}{\\sigma^{2}(b\_{T}(t))}, |  |\
\
for a sequence (tn,xn)→(t,bT​(t))(t\_{n},x\_{n})\\to(t,b\_{T}(t)) with (tn,xn)∈𝒞T(t\_{n},x\_{n})\\in\\mathcal{C}\_{T} for all n∈ℕn\\in\\mathbb{N}. Now,\
combining this with the fact that ∂𝒟T⊂𝒮T∖𝒰¯\\partial\\mathcal{D}\_{T}\\subset\\mathcal{S}\_{T}\\setminus\\overline{\\mathcal{U}}, where 𝒰¯\\overline{\\mathcal{U}} denotes the closure of 𝒰\\mathcal{U} in 𝒮\\mathcal{S}, we obtain\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | g′′​(bT​(t))>−2​c​(t)σ2​(bT​(t)).g^{\\prime\\prime}(b\_{T}(t))>-\\frac{2c(t)}{\\sigma^{2}(b\_{T}(t))}. |  |\
\
Thus, ∂x​xVT\\partial\_{xx}V\_{T} has a discontinuity across the boundary.\
∎\
\
With the smooth-fit property of the value function, Lipschitz stopping boundaries, and the separation of the boundary and the 𝒰\\mathcal{U} set, we can upgrade to continuously differentiable optimal stopping boundaries by \[ [10](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#bib.bib10 ""), Theorem 2.4\], thereby proving Corollary [4.4](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S4.Thmtheorem4 "Corollary 4.4. ‣ 4. Main Results ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales").\
\
###### Proof of Corollary [4.4](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1\#S4.Thmtheorem4 "Corollary 4.4. ‣ 4. Main Results ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales").\
\
The result follows if we can verify \[ [10](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#bib.bib10 ""), Assumption 2.3\]. Their problem is formulated as a maximization problem, and we implicitly use the standard conversion from minimization with running cost to maximization with terminal gain.\
Take any t0∈(0,T)t\_{0}\\in(0,T). By Theorem [4.1](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S4.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem 4.1 (Structure of the continuation region). ‣ 4. Main Results ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales") giving γ⁡(t)<bT​(t)<1\\gamma(t)<b\_{T}(t)<1 for all t<Tt<T it holds that there exists an open rectangle ℛ\\mathcal{R} with closure ℛ¯⊂\[0,T)×(0,1)\\overline{\\mathcal{R}}\\subset\[0,T)\\times(0,1) and (t0,bT​(t0))∈ℛ(t\_{0},b\_{T}(t\_{0}))\\in\\mathcal{R} such that: (i) VTV\_{T} is continuously differentiable in time and space on ℛ¯\\overline{\\mathcal{R}}, by Theorem [4.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S4.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 4.2 (Regularity and smooth fit). ‣ 4. Main Results ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales"), (ii) with some abuse of notation, g∈C2​(ℛ¯)g\\in C^{2}(\\overline{\\mathcal{R}}), c∈C1​(ℛ¯)c\\in C^{1}(\\overline{\\mathcal{R}}), and h⁡(t,x)=c⁡(t)+ℒ​g​(x)>0h(t,x)=c(t)+\\mathcal{L}g(x)>0, by Assumption [B.(i)](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S3.I2.i1 "In Assumption B (Extended assumptions). ‣ 3.2. Assumptions ‣ 3. Setup and Preliminary Analysis ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales") on gg, Assumption [A.(ii)](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S3.I1.i2 "In Assumption A (Baseline assumptions). ‣ 3.2. Assumptions ‣ 3. ## 7\. Free-Boundary Integral Equation\
\
To get an explicit equation for the boundary in both the finite- and infinite-horizon cases, we can utilize the previous results to obtain a nonlinear integral equation under Assumption [B](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#S3.Thmtheorem0.Thmassumptionalt2 "Assumption B (Extended assumptions). ‣ 3.2. Assumptions ‣ 3. Setup and Preliminary Analysis ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales"), which we will assume throughout the section. We treat the finite- and infinite-horizon cases simultaneously, and we obtain uniqueness in both cases. For any function f:\[0,T)→(1/2,1)f:\[0,T)\\to(1/2,1), we define the family of sets\
\
|     |     |     |\
| --- | --- | --- |\
|  | 𝒞tf:={x:x∈(1−f⁡(t),f⁡(t))}\\mathcal{C}^{f}\_{t}:=\\{x:x\\in(1-f(t),f(t))\\} |  |\
\
for t∈\[0,T)t\\in\[0,T). In the proof below we suppress the TT notation for bTb\_{T}. Note in particular that 𝒞tbT={x∈(0,1):(t,x)∈𝒞T}\\mathcal{C}^{b\_{T}}\_{t}=\\{x\\in(0,1):(t,x)\\in\\mathcal{C}\_{T}\\}.\

###### Proof of Theorem [4.5](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1\#S4.Thmtheorem5 "Theorem 4.5 (Free-boundary integral equation). ‣ 4. Main Results ‣ When should one stop the most exciting game?Sequential Inference for win-martingales").\

On every compact time interval, the curves bb and 1−b1-b are separated and of bounded variation. Moreover, VTV\_{T} is C1,2C^{1,2} off the curves\
and the spatial concavity gives the local variation condition in \[ [27](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.12291v1#bib.bib27 ""), Remark 3.2\].
